# Lightagent Desktop — build plan and Claude execution prompt

Prepared October 29, 2026. Repository: https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent

## Purpose and evidence boundary

Build an independently installed Lightagent application with a WebUI-style interface and complete coverage of existing CLI capabilities, including tools, providers, skills, and other existing functions. The application must start its bundled runtime without requiring a terminal or a separately started WebUI. Remote server connections are a secondary mode.

This is a proposed implementation plan, not a completed source audit. Repository access was unavailable when preparing it. Existing command names, APIs, features, supported OS versions, current releases, and framework compatibility must be verified from the current repository. Do not use similarly named LightAgent projects as evidence.

## Target architecture

Use one shared Lightagent application/service layer beneath CLI, WebUI, and Desktop. Retain current behavior and reuse existing backend operations. Extract missing shared operations incrementally; avoid a wholesale runtime rewrite. Prefer the existing WebUI frontend where technically appropriate.

The desktop host owns window lifecycle, packaged assets, native integration, and the bundled runtime lifecycle. A narrow typed IPC or authenticated local transport connects the frontend to the application service. Do not implement desktop features by scraping human-readable CLI output or constructing arbitrary shell command strings. A structured, versioned CLI transport is acceptable only if the audit shows it is the safest reusable boundary.

Evaluate Tauri and Electron against the real frontend, Rust integration, process model, Intel Mac support, platform dependencies, security, memory use, packaging, accessibility, and maintenance. Tauri is a candidate, not a predetermined decision. Record an architecture decision and choose one before building the shell. Any measurements must come from GitHub-hosted runners and be labeled as runner measurements.

Local mode bundles the application runtime, not model weights or every external MCP/tool dependency. Explain required external executables and provider connectivity clearly. Remote mode never silently executes remote file operations on the desktop machine; show the active endpoint, profile, workspace, and execution location.

## Mandatory execution policy

- Use GitHub Actions/Workflows on GitHub-hosted runners for all builds, formatting, linting, type checking, rendering, tests, benchmarks, dependency validation, packaging, installer checks, and release dry-runs. No self-hosted runners, local CI emulators, local app launches, or local validation commands.
- Local machines are restricted to repository copying/safekeeping, static source reading, and writing progress/plan documentation. Do not locally edit implementation source or workflows. Make implementation changes through GitHub-native branch/file APIs or an explicitly configured GitHub-hosted Actions editing workflow. Never silently relax this rule for convenience.
- If the available remote editing mechanism cannot complete a required operation, document the precise blocker and request a narrow policy clarification. Do not substitute local builds, tests, or source edits.
- This prompt authorizes development branches, draft PRs, source changes through the permitted remote mechanism, and necessary GitHub-hosted CI runs. It does not authorize merging, tagging, release publication, deployment, production activation, or automatic updates to installed applications.
- Keep existing work intact. Read applicable AGENTS.md and repository contribution/security instructions. Report any conflict with this policy before performing the conflicting action.
- Never include secrets in source, logs, screenshots, artifacts, fixtures, or progress documentation. Use deterministic fake providers and MCP servers for routine verification. Do not use paid/live services or production credentials without separate authorization.

## Structured work order and gates

All slices below are proposed PR scopes, not actual PR numbers. Maintain dependency order. A dependent slice may build on an unmerged branch, but cannot be declared integration-validated until its exact combined tree has passed the relevant CI. Do not merge any slice under this prompt.

| Order | Slice | Work and required deliverables | Exit gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| D0 | Baseline and feature audit | Record default branch/full SHA/tree; inspect CLI parser, interactive commands, configuration, runtime, WebUI, APIs, extensions, and CI. Inventory every command/subcommand/flag and user-facing operation. | Source-backed inventory; existing versus planned features distinguished; current baseline Actions evidence or clearly recorded blockers. |
| D1 | Architecture and UI specification | Decide framework and backend boundary; document process ownership, configuration compatibility, data paths, credential handling, local/remote semantics, minimum OS/architecture targets, screen map, and parity mapping. | Architecture decision, threat model, wireframes, dependency graph, and measurable acceptance criteria. |
| D2 | CI and desktop skeleton | Add remote-only build/test/render workflow; minimal packaged shell; mocked backend; version handshake; artifact metadata; frontend/backend contract generation or validation. | Shell builds and renders on declared targets; CI fails on incompatible contracts; screenshots and logs saved as Actions artifacts. |
| D3 | Shared service and runtime lifecycle | Expose/reuse typed operations; bundle matching runtime; readiness handshake, cancellation, bounded logs, shutdown, crash handling, single-instance policy, connection recovery, and version mismatch reporting. | Service contract and lifecycle tests pass; clean launch requires no developer toolchain or terminal; only owned processes are stopped. |
| D4 | Profiles, providers, credentials, workspaces | Wire real configuration operations, provider/model selection, endpoint testing, profile isolation, workspace selection, supported settings, atomic writes, and migration backup. | CLI/Desktop/WebUI share intended state; concurrent writes are safe; secret read failures fail closed; credentials never appear in renderer state or diagnostics. |
| D5 | Sessions and agent execution | Streaming chat, history, run status, cancellation, tool requests/results, approvals, errors, usage/budgets where implemented, and reconnect reconciliation. | Deterministic end-to-end run survives relevant lifecycle cases without duplicate execution or lost approvals; failures remain visible. |
| D6 | Tools, MCP, extensions, and skills | Provide source-backed management and execution controls for each existing capability. Support existing local/repository install mechanisms; clearly identify any genuinely missing backend operation as additional scoped work. | Installation/activation/removal and permission flows verified with fixtures; untrusted skill text cannot bypass application permissions; external dependencies/errors are visible. |
| D7 | Remaining CLI parity | Wire all remaining inventoried operations: memory/RAG, agents/subagents, scheduling, diagnostics, import/export, provisioning, or other areas only where present. Use dedicated controls or typed command-palette forms. | Every inventory row has a usable UI path and evidence; destructive/admin operations retain existing restrictions; no concealed unsupported items. |
| D8 | Remote mode and regression hardening | Endpoint authentication, transport restrictions, local/remote scope labels, capability negotiation, reconnect, concurrency, accessibility, keyboard use, and existing CLI/WebUI regression coverage. | Remote failures do not trigger silent local execution; version/capability mismatch is explicit; security and regression checks pass. |
| D9 | Installers and release dry-run | Build declared platform packages; verify bundled runtime and dependency requirements, clean-user launch, upgrade/uninstall behavior, checksums, licenses, SBOM, and vulnerability gates. | Exact installer artifacts tested where runner environment supports it; missing interactive/native tests explicitly block affected readiness claims. No publish/tag. |
| D10 | Review and approval package | Reconcile parity inventory, review changes, resolve defects, validate exact integration tree and proposed dependency-ordered merge sequence, provide recovery notes and complete evidence. | Ready-for-review report with unresolved risks and separate merge/release approval boundaries. Stop before merge. |

Required dependency order: D0 → D1 → D2 → D3 → D4 → D5 → D6 → D7 → D8 → D9 → D10. Split oversized slices into numbered child PRs with explicit prerequisites. Refine dependencies after the audit while preserving foundation-before-consumer order. Existing rolling-summary or other feature branches are dependencies only if actually required; do not silently absorb unrelated work.

## CLI parity contract

Create a machine-readable capability inventory and a readable matrix. Use stable IDs and include: source location, command/subcommand/option or interactive operation, behavior, backend operation, configuration/data scope, local/remote applicability, required permission, UI screen/action, implementation status, verification type, exact tested SHA, and Actions run/artifact link.

Include non-command entry points such as slash commands, interactive prompts, environment-driven behavior, config-only features, extension registrations, and relevant API operations. A command palette counts only when it presents supported typed inputs, invokes the real operation, and displays structured output. An embedded terminal or a generic command textbox does not establish parity.

For every row, distinguish implemented, unsupported with reason, intentionally deferred, and not applicable. Freeze the initial inventory at the audited SHA and check newly added commands for mapping drift in CI. Report numerator/denominator and excluded items; do not claim 100% while any applicable existing operation remains unwired. New functions such as GitHub skill installation must be labeled new scope if the current backend does not support them.

## UI organization

Reuse established WebUI language and navigation where practical. Proposed areas: Conversations; Runs/Agents; Providers/Models; Tools/MCP/Extensions; Skills; Memory/Knowledge; Profiles/Workspaces; Settings/Diagnostics. Show unavailable capabilities with a reason rather than nonfunctional controls. Preserve keyboard navigation, accessible labels, readable errors, and a clear indication of where tools will execute. Render mockups and final screens only in GitHub Actions.

## Backend and desktop requirements

- Define request/response/event schemas with correlation IDs, stable error categories, run IDs, sequence handling, cancellation semantics, and a version/capability handshake. Reconcile authoritative state after event gaps; do not assume uninterrupted streaming.
- Preserve the existing configuration and secret-store authority where secure. Do not introduce a second unsynchronized credential store. Restrict secret access to the backend; show only redacted status in the UI. Any migration requires backup, atomic update, failure recovery, and documented compatibility.
- If using an HTTP sidecar, bind locally by default, use an unpredictable per-launch credential, validate origins/hosts as appropriate, avoid credentials in URLs, and prevent unauthenticated local access. If using IPC, expose narrowly scoped commands with validated inputs.
- Disable unnecessary renderer privileges and remote content execution. Validate file paths, archive extraction, subprocess arguments, external links, and dependency installation. Keep shell/tool permissions in the shared runtime; UI text must not grant authority.
- Keep CLI, WebUI, and Desktop behavior consistent by calling shared services. Avoid multiple writers corrupting session/configuration data. Define locks/transactions and ownership of active runs.
- On app close, distinguish owned local processes from external/shared servers. Warn or follow a documented policy for active runs; never kill an unrelated Lightagent server.
- Record package/runtime/protocol versions. Use the bundled compatible runtime by default; do not automatically select an arbitrary binary on PATH. Updates remain manual unless separately designed and approved.

## Verification strategy

Use meaningful service/contract tests, CLI regression tests, frontend component tests for important interaction states, and deterministic packaged end-to-end tests. Exercise fake provider streaming, tool calls, approvals/denials, cancellation, reconnect, malformed responses, unavailable endpoints, permission failures, and process crashes. Test that equivalent CLI and desktop operations produce equivalent persistent state where applicable.

Test configuration migration and concurrent access with isolated temporary homes. Cover secret-store unavailable/locked/unreadable conditions using fixtures; use platform credential integration tests only where safely supported. Check that errors and artifacts remain redacted.

For skills/extensions, verify source identity and revision, installation boundaries, path traversal rejection, failure recovery, duplicate handling, and explicit executable/dependency setup. Do not claim arbitrary GitHub repositories are compatible without format validation.

Define the supported target matrix from evidence. Prioritize an Intel macOS package for the user's Mac, then Apple Silicon macOS, Windows x64, and Linux x64 as feasible. Verify minimum supported OS versions and availability of GitHub-hosted runner architectures before promising coverage. A cross-compiled binary is not proof it runs on that architecture or on an older macOS release. If runner coverage is unavailable, mark that gate blocked and propose an allowed hosted alternative; never substitute local validation.

Measure packaged startup, idle memory, installer size, and stream responsiveness on named runners. Agree thresholds in D1 based on evidence rather than inventing performance claims. Perform installer smoke tests under a clean user/environment without development tools on PATH. Separately report build success, packaged launch success, native UI coverage, signing/notarization status, and supported OS evidence.

Signing/notarization requiring credentials or an externally visible submission is a separate authorization boundary. Unsigned CI artifacts can support review but must not be presented as a polished publicly distributable release. Never bypass existing security gates or retry a flaky test until green without retaining and investigating failures.

## Progress and review records

Proposed documentation names (adapt to repository conventions): DESKTOP_BUILD_PLAN.md, DESKTOP_ARCHITECTURE.md, DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md, desktop-capabilities.json, DESKTOP_PROGRESS.md, DESKTOP_ACCEPTANCE.md, and DESKTOP_MERGE_ORDER.md.

Record each slice's status, changed behavior, branch/PR, full head and tested SHA/tree, dependencies, Actions links, checks, artifacts, failures, policy exceptions, open risks, and next step. Separate proposed, implemented, CI-validated, packaged-tested, and approved states. Preserve the existing repository copy locally for safekeeping and keep progress documentation current; do not store secrets or compiled artifacts there as a workaround.

Before requesting merge approval, list exact PR heads, proposed order, prospective combined trees, validation evidence, migration/recovery notes, and blockers. Validate proposed merge results on GitHub-hosted runners. If an approved head later changes, that approval no longer covers it. Future merges require explicit approval and validation of the resulting default-branch tree at each approved boundary. Release/tag/publish permission is separate from merge permission.

## Definition of done

The installed application launches independently, starts its own compatible runtime, and exposes every applicable existing CLI capability through working desktop actions. Shared data and behavior remain compatible with CLI/WebUI; local/remote scope is explicit; credentials and permissions remain enforced. All declared packages have the stated hosted-runner evidence, the parity inventory has no unexplained gaps, and documentation accurately states limitations. Completion under this prompt means a validated review package and installer artifacts—not a merged or published release.

# Copy-and-paste prompt for Claude

You are implementing Lightagent Desktop for https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent. Follow the complete build plan above as the governing task specification. If this prompt is pasted separately, attach this entire document and read it before starting.

Goal: create an independently installable desktop application similar to the existing WebUI, with the complete existing CLI functionality wired into working UI actions: tools, providers, models, skills, MCP/extensions, agents, sessions, memory, settings, and every other capability confirmed by the repository. Reuse the same backend logic across Desktop, CLI, and WebUI. Do not assume that proposed features already exist.

NON-NEGOTIABLE EXECUTION RULES:
1. All implementation editing must use GitHub-native branch/file APIs or an explicitly configured GitHub-hosted Actions editing workflow. Local machines are ONLY for repo copies/safekeeping, static source reading, and progress documentation. No local implementation/workflow edits, builds, launches, renders, formatting, linting, type checking, tests, packaging, dependency installation, or release dry-runs. No self-hosted runners or local CI emulation. If a required permitted editing path is unavailable, report the blocker; do not improvise a local exception.
2. Use GitHub Actions/Workflows and GitHub-hosted runners exclusively for validation and artifact production. Keep workflow permissions minimal and secrets protected. Use isolated fake services by default.
3. You may create development branches/draft PRs and run necessary hosted CI. Do NOT merge, tag, publish a release, deploy, activate production, submit externally visible signing/notarization work, or change installed user applications without separate explicit approval.
4. Preserve existing work and read applicable AGENTS.md/contribution/security instructions. Do not include unrelated refactors or silently pull in another project or feature stack.
5. Keep progress documentation with exact SHA/tree and Actions evidence. Never call predicted results verified; never claim parity from a terminal wrapper or from build success alone.

WORK ORDER:
Execute D0 through D10 in the dependency order specified above. Begin with repository inspection, not framework installation or UI scaffolding. First identify the actual default branch/full SHA/tree, existing CLI/WebUI architecture, active overlapping work, current workflow health, and supported targets. Build the complete capability inventory from source. Then choose the desktop framework and shared service boundary in a written architecture decision. Refine the proposed PR scopes and dependencies using the audit, and continue implementation in small reviewable draft PRs once the relevant prerequisite gates pass. Do not stop merely because a routine design choice needs judgment. Stop only for a genuine authorization boundary, conflicting requirements, missing access, unsafe migration, or a failed prerequisite you cannot resolve within scope.

For each slice: implement through the permitted remote mechanism; run appropriate Actions checks; inspect every result; fix failures; update progress; report its evidence and continue to the next dependent slice. Use a combined integration branch/tree as needed, without merging the PRs. Any workflow that creates commits must identify the final tested commit, avoid uncontrolled self-triggering, and preserve auditable provenance.

FEATURE COVERAGE:
Maintain a source-backed parity matrix for all CLI commands/subcommands/options, interactive and slash operations, configuration behavior, and related existing capabilities. Each row needs a real backend operation, UI path, permissions/scope, verification, and exact Actions evidence. Keep added features distinct from existing parity requirements. Implement typed controls or a typed command palette for advanced operations; arbitrary command execution is not coverage. Do not defer existing capabilities silently or call a partial app complete.

SECURITY AND LIFECYCLE:
Use a bundled compatible runtime, secure narrow IPC/local transport, existing secret authority, explicit local/remote scope, safe configuration writes, process ownership, cancellation, crash recovery, and event reconciliation. Do not expose backend credentials to the renderer. Do not silently fall back from a failed remote connection to local tool execution. Keep external dependencies visible and retain runtime permission enforcement.

VALIDATION:
Follow the plan's service, contract, frontend, parity, regression, lifecycle, security, migration, and packaged end-to-end gates. Produce declared platform installers and checksums on hosted runners. Prioritize the Intel Mac target but verify minimum OS compatibility; do not infer runtime compatibility from cross-compilation. Mark unsupported hosted-runner/native UI coverage explicitly. No live paid-provider calls or production credentials without authorization.

FINAL HANDOFF:
Provide the complete capability inventory, architecture decision, implemented/deferred/blocked counts, draft PRs in dependency order with exact heads, exact tested integration tree, Actions links and artifacts, platform/package evidence, unresolved risks, recovery instructions, and a concrete merge approval package. State clearly that nothing was merged, tagged, published, or deployed. Stop at that boundary. Do not ask for merge approval until the reviewable work and required validation are complete, or explain precisely why a blocker prevents completion.

START NOW with D0 and the source-backed audit. Treat old conversation SHAs, version numbers, API names, and this plan's proposed framework/paths as unverified until checked against the current repository. Your first progress report should identify the baseline, audit findings, permitted remote editing method, proposed framework decision process, concrete work order, and any access blockers—without claiming implementation has already passed.

The repository owner's execution instructions for Lightagent Desktop, committed
verbatim in the order they were given. The original copy-and-paste prompt is

## 1. Continuation after the first report (2026-10-29)

Continue with the existing Lightagent Desktop build plan. This first-report checkpoint does not require another approval. You are authorized to create development branches, draft PRs, and run necessary GitHub-hosted Actions within the plan.

1. Finish D0 before declaring its gate passed:
   - Inventory individual flags, chat key bindings, ACP methods, MCP/extension registration, and setup options.
   - Give each capability a stable ID, source reference, desktop applicability, and intended UI/backend mapping.
   - Reconcile overlapping CLI/API/config entries so the coverage denominator is clear.
   - Keep the six unresolved applicability decisions explicit until D1 resolves them.
   - Record exact baseline Actions links. Explain the deliberate fixture failures with workflow/test evidence; do not simply exclude unexplained failures.

2. Use the existing remote source-edit workflow to create the audit branch and draft PR. Verify its fixed base, resulting commit, branch update, and provenance. The drift check must detect additions to the inventoried command structure and document any coverage it cannot automatically detect.

3. Complete D1 before committing to a desktop framework:
   - Compare Tauri and Electron against the repository dependency policy, existing React frontend, packaging, process lifecycle, and supported operating systems.
   - Treat macOS 10.14 compatibility as an unverified target. Distinguish Intel architecture support from minimum macOS support, and distinguish build success from runtime evidence.
   - Decide profile switching explicitly, including active runs, locks, credentials, event streams, and failure recovery.
   - Define how the desktop launch credential reaches the UI without placing it in URLs, persistent browser storage, logs, or artifacts.
   - Address authentication, Host validation, Origin validation, and event-stream authentication together. A random API key alone does not complete the local-server security design.

4. Continue D2 through D10 in dependency order once prerequisite gates pass. Keep PRs small and maintain the exact proposed merge order. Do not stop after routine progress reports.

Preserve the execution policy:
- GitHub-native APIs or the GitHub-hosted source-edit workflow for implementation changes.

- Local machines only for repository safekeeping, static reading, and progress documentation.
- No merge, tag, release publication, deployment, production activation, or installed-app updates without separate explicit approval.

Stop only for a genuine blocker or authorization boundary. Otherwise continue through implementation and validation, ending with the complete review package and exact tested heads/trees.

EXECUTION BOUNDARIES

- Local machine: repository copying/synchronization, safekeeping, static source reading, and progress documentation ONLY.

# Lightagent Desktop — progress

Governing documents: [DESKTOP_BUILD_PLAN.md](DESKTOP_BUILD_PLAN.md) and
[DESKTOP_CLAUDE_PROMPT.md](DESKTOP_CLAUDE_PROMPT.md).

States: **proposed** → **implemented** (committed on a branch) →
**CI-validated** (a named hosted run passed on that exact commit) →
**packaged-tested** → **approved**. Nothing has been merged, tagged,
published, or deployed.

A commit cannot record its own SHA. Each update records evidence for commits
that already exist; the commit carrying an update is itself untested until the
next update names its run.

## Status board

| Slice | State | Branch | Draft PR | Latest implementation commit | Latest tested commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D0 Baseline and feature audit | implemented; validation pending | `desktop/d0-audit` | pending | (this commit's parent chain; see the D0 log) | none yet |
| D1 Architecture and UI specification | not started | — | — | — | — |
| D2–D10 | not started | — | — | — | — |

## Execution method

- **Editing:** the existing `source-edit` workflow on the never-merged
  `tools/source-edit` branch. An edit request pinned to a base SHA is applied
  on a GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04` runner, which runs `cargo fmt` and creates
  a commit through the git data API without moving any branch. Blobs for
  workflow files are created there. The final commit, which attaches them, is
  created through the git data API under the owner's token (scopes `repo`,
  `workflow`). A `desktop/*` branch ref is then created or fast-forwarded to
  it, never forced.
- **Validation:** `.github/workflows/desktop.yml` (this slice) plus the
  existing `check` workflow, on GitHub-hosted runners only.
- **Local machine:** a repository backup and these documents only. No local
  builds, tests, or source edits.

## D0 — Baseline and feature audit

### Baseline

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Repository | `dlroqa/Lightagent` |
| Default branch | `master` |
| Audited (frozen) SHA | `12582a046ba37a7fd59c86c1d4fb3a911fa7564a` (merge of PR #89, `release/v0.5.0-dry-run`) |
| Audited tree | `59b8f136534d228e328ef6795c26f63de6f47faa` |
| Version | 0.5.0; Rust 1.98.0 pinned (`rust-toolchain.toml`); edition 2024 |
| Latest release | v0.5.0 |
| Open PRs at audit | none |
| Overlapping desktop work | none: no branch or file referenced a desktop shell, Tauri, or Electron |
| AGENTS.md / CONTRIBUTING / SECURITY | absent. The binding repository policy is the dependency policy in `Cargo.toml` (no cmake, no OpenSSL, no libclang; polars/tonic/sqlx banned), gated by `scripts/check-deps.sh`, plus `scripts/check-secrets.sh` |

### Baseline Actions evidence (audited SHA)

| Workflow | Run | Result |
|---|---|---|
| check | https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent/actions/runs/40975649064 | success: 13 jobs, including check on macos-arm64 (`macos-15`), macos-x64 (`macos-15-intel`), windows-x64, and linux-x64; Web UI render; package smoke; release gate |
| jev evaluation | https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent/actions/runs/40975649134 | success |
| subagents | https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent/actions/runs/40975721175 | success |
| subagent fixture (pass) | https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent/actions/runs/40975766850 | success (expected) |
| subagent fixture (fail) | https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent/actions/runs/40975789839 | failure (**expected**) |
| subagent fixture (verify) | https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent/actions/runs/40975863447 | failure (**expected**) |
| release (v0.5.0) | https://github.com/dlroqa/Lightagent/actions/runs/40976901096 | success |

**Why the two fixture failures are deliberate:**
`.github/workflows/subagent-fixture.yml` (header comment, lines 3-10) is a
target for the `subagents` tester broker's live check. In `mode: fail`, its
one step runs `echo "FAIL: deliberate fixture failure"; exit 1`. In
`mode: verify`, it compiles and tests `fixtures/subagents/coder/lib.rs`, whose
test `tests::one_is_one` deliberately fails. The log of run 40975863447 shows
`panicked at fixtures/subagents/coder/lib.rs:14:9: assertion left == right
failed` and `test result: FAILED. 0 passed; 1 failed`. The broker must report
each fixture's outcome as it is. The `subagents` run that dispatched them
(40975721175) passed, so the broker reported them correctly. Neither failure
is a baseline defect.

### Audit findings that shape D1–D3

1. **The process model exists.** `lightagent serve` (`crates/lightagent/src/serve.rs:732`)
   serves `/api/lightagent/v1` (21 method/route pairs, `crates/lightagent-api/src/lib.rs:73-112`)
   and the React 19 + Vite WebUI from `--web-root` (`frontend/`; screens `/`, `/tools`, `/settings`).
2. **The API covers only part of the CLI.** It has no operation for config
   path/get/set and the Jev routing report, models, profile
   show/create/use/preset/delete, the extensions commands, worker
   assign/revoke, memory, RAG, identity, remote credentials, import, doctor,
   init/setup, onboard, reload, **continue** (resume after the time budget), or
   **steering** (input queued while a run is in progress).
3. **CLI handlers are print-bound.** They have the shape
   `fn(args…, json: bool) -> Result<(), String>` and print their results
   (for example `extensions.rs:27`, `rag.rs:697`, `import.rs:28`, `memory.rs`).
   Typed shared operations must be extracted (D3) so CLI, API, and desktop call
   the same code.
4. **Loopback is unauthenticated.** `serve.rs:746-758`: with no `--key-env`,
   a loopback bind uses `AuthConfig::open()`. `crates/lightagent-api` has no
   Host or Origin validation, so a DNS-rebinding page or any local process can
   drive tools. This is an input to the D1 security design.
5. **One profile per server process.** `AppState.session_profile` is fixed
   at start (`serve.rs:739-760`). `POST /runs {profile}` is honoured only in
   multi-user mode (`lib.rs:1148-1160`). The active-profile write
   `ProfileStore::set_active` is atomic (temp file + rename,
   `paths.rs:173-185`), but no lock coordinates a running server with a CLI
   that switches profile. This is an input to the D1 profile-switch decision.
6. **Secret authority.** Provider keys are stored in owner-only
   `~/.lightagent/secrets` and referenced as `SecretRef`. The
   remote-credential vault is sealed with
   `LIGHTAGENT_CREDENTIAL_KEY[_FILE]`. Identity secrets have strict delivery
   rules (`identity_admin.rs:10-14`).
7. **Targets and runners.** `check.yml` and `release.yml` already run
   natively on `macos-15-intel` (x86_64, `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=10.14`),
   `macos-15` (arm64, 11.0), `windows-latest`, and `ubuntu-22.04`/`24.04`.
   The 10.14 floor is a declared deployment target; no hosted runner executes
   macOS older than 15. It stays an unverified target (D1).

### Capability inventory

- Files: [desktop-capabilities.json](desktop-capabilities.json) (rows),
  [cli-tree.txt](cli-tree.txt) (pinned clap tree), and
  [DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md](DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md) (readable matrix).
- 306 rows: 86 commands, 56 options, 47 positional arguments, 6 setup-section
  values, 2 aliases, 10 setup options, 16 slash commands, 6 key bindings,
  8 ACP methods, 10 MCP registration fields, 6 extension registration rows,
  1 skill registration row, 14 config sections, 12 environment variables, and
  26 API operations.
- **Parity denominator: 225** applicable, counted rows. **Implemented: 0 of 225.**
- Rows that do not count toward parity:
  - groups, and commands that run a default subcommand;
  - aliases;
  - 26 rows marked `duplicate_of` (for example `/tools` → `cli.tools.list`,
    and ACP methods → Conversations rows);
  - 22 not-applicable rows, each with a reason;
  - 5 host-owned `serve` rows;
  - backend-only API rows. Four API rows count because the WebUI exposes them
    with no CLI equivalent: Jev status/verify, session search, session
    rename/pin, and attachments.
- **Six open applicability decisions** (8 rows), resolved in D1:
  - D1-Q1: `update` and its `--check` and `--force` options;
  - D1-Q2: `/update`;
  - D1-Q3–Q5: the three feature-gated provisioning routes;
  - D1-Q6: `LIGHTAGENT_INSTALL_ROOT`.
- New backend operations required for existing CLI capabilities (not new
  scope): **continue** and **steering** (D5), and typed MCP server
  configuration (D6).

### Drift check

- `desktop_inventory` (Rust test in `crates/lightagent/src/lib.rs`) walks
  `Cli::command()` and requires an exact match with `cli-tree.txt`. Any added,
  removed, or renamed command, option, argument, setup value, or alias fails it.
- `scripts/check-desktop-inventory.py` statically checks:
  - slash words, chat key codes, and ACP methods;
  - MCP transports and fields, and extension manifest fields;
  - config sections and HTTP routes;
  - literal environment names;
  - the inventory schema and denominator, and the parity matrix.

  `--self-test` injects one addition into each surface and requires the check
  to report it.
- It cannot detect setup menu items, new modifier combinations, behaviour
  changes behind unchanged names, config keys below the section level, or
  computed environment names. These are listed in DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md.

## Open applicability decisions (resolved in D1)

| Decision | Rows | Question |
|---|---|---|
| D1-Q1 | `cli.update`, `cli.update.opt.check`, `cli.update.opt.force` | Does the desktop expose CLI self-update (`lightagent update`)? The plan forbids automatic updates of installed apps. |
| D1-Q2 | `slash.update` | Same as D1-Q1 for the /update slash command. |
| D1-Q3 | `api.provisioning.put.profiles` | Is feature-gated profile provisioning a desktop capability? |
| D1-Q4 | `api.provisioning.get.operations` | Provisioning operation status in desktop? |
| D1-Q5 | `api.provisioning.post.operations` | Provisioning revoke in desktop? |
| D1-Q6 | `env.LIGHTAGENT_INSTALL_ROOT` | Does the desktop honour or expose the update install root? |

## What the drift check cannot detect automatically

- Setup menu items inside a section (`setup.*` rows) — reviewed by hand when `crates/lightagent/src/setup.rs` changes.
- New modifier combinations or changed meaning of an existing key in `chat.rs` (new `KeyCode` variants are detected).
- Behaviour changes behind an unchanged command, flag, route, or field name.
- Config keys below the section level (sections are detected; keys are covered by the settings forms in D4).
- Environment variables read through a computed name (literal names are detected).

   "tokens": [
    "POST /api/lightagent/v1/provisioning/operations/{operation_id}/revoke"
   ],
   "applicability": "decision-D1",
   "decision": "D1-Q5",
   "reason": null,
   "counts_toward_parity": true,
   "duplicate_of": null,
   "ui": null,
   "backend": "api (feature provisioning-api)",
   "permission": "admin-scope",
   "data_scope": "home",
   "desktop_status": "not-started",
   "verification": null,
   "tested_sha": null,
   "actions_evidence": null
  }
 ]
}

=== write scripts/check-desktop-inventory.py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Keep the Lightagent Desktop capability inventory in step with the source.

docs/desktop/desktop-capabilities.json lists every user-facing capability the
desktop must map. The CLI command tree is pinned in docs/desktop/cli-tree.txt
and compared with clap itself by the `desktop_inventory` test in
crates/lightagent/src/lib.rs. This script checks everything else statically:

  * the inventory's own shape (unique ids, known enums, resolvable duplicates);
  * every cli-tree.txt line has exactly one inventory row, and no row names a
    line that is not in the tree;
  * slash-command words, chat key codes, ACP methods, MCP transports and their
    fields, extension manifest fields, config sections, HTTP routes, and
    literal LIGHTAGENT_* environment names found in the source all have rows,
    and no row names one the source no longer has;
  * DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md lists every row and states the current denominator.

What it cannot see is listed in DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md ("What the drift check
cannot detect automatically"). `--self-test` adds one item to each scanned
surface in memory and requires the check to fail for each.
"""

import json
import os
import re
import sys

ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
DOCS = os.path.join(ROOT, "docs", "desktop")
SOURCES = {
    "slash": "crates/lightagent/src/slash.rs",
    "chat": "crates/lightagent/src/chat.rs",
    "acp": "crates/lightagent-acp/src/server.rs",
    "config": "crates/lightagent-core/src/config.rs",
    "extensions": "crates/lightagent-extensions/src/lib.rs",
    "api": "crates/lightagent-api/src/lib.rs",
    "provisioning": "crates/lightagent-api/src/provisioning.rs",
}
KINDS = {"command", "option", "argument", "value", "alias", "setup-option", "slash", "keybinding", "acp-method",
         "mcp-registration", "extension-registration", "skill-registration", "config", "env", "api"}
APPLICABILITY = {"applicable", "not-applicable", "host-owned", "decision-D1"}
STATUS = {"not-started", "implemented", "unsupported", "deferred", "not-applicable"}
REQUIRED = ("id", "kind", "surface", "source", "behavior", "applicability", "counts_toward_parity", "desktop_status")


def read(rel):
    with open(os.path.join(ROOT, rel), encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        return handle.read()


def before_tests(text):
    """The text before the first `#[cfg(test)]` module."""
    return text.split("#[cfg(test)]", 1)[0]


def block(text, start):
    """The brace-balanced block opening at the first `{` after `start`."""
    at = text.index(start)
    open_at = text.index("{", at)
    depth = 0
    for index in range(open_at, len(text)):
        if text[index] == "{":
            depth += 1
        elif text[index] == "}":
            depth -= 1
            if depth == 0:
                return text[open_at + 1:index]
    raise ValueError(f"unbalanced block after {start!r}")


def scan_slash(text):
    body = block(text, "pub fn parse(")
    words = set()
    for head in re.findall(r'^\s*((?:"[^"]*"\s*\|\s*)*"[^"]*")\s*=>', body, re.M):
        words.update(re.findall(r'"([^"]*)"', head))
    sub = set(re.findall(r'args == "(\w+)"', body)) | set(re.findall(r'strip_prefix\("(\w+) "\)', body))
    return words | sub


def scan_keys(text):
    code = before_tests(text)
    keys = set()
    for name, char in re.findall(r"KeyCode::(\w+)(?:\('(.)'\))?", code):
        keys.add(f"Char('{char}')" if char else name)
    return keys


def scan_acp(text):
    code = before_tests(text)
    # Dispatch arms open a block; value mappings (`"user" => "…"`) do not.
    methods = set(re.findall(r'^\s*"([a-z_]+(?:/[a-z_]+)?)"\s*=>\s*\{', code, re.M))
    methods |= set(re.findall(r'"(session/[a-z_]+)"', code))
    return methods


def scan_mcp(text):
    body = block(text, "pub enum McpServerEntry")
    found = set()
    for variant, fields in re.findall(r"^\s{4}(\w+)\s*\{(.*?)^\s{4}\}", body, re.M | re.S):
        lower = variant.lower()
        found.add(lower)
        for field in re.findall(r"^\s{8}(\w+):", fields, re.M):
            if field != "name":
                found.add(f"{lower}.{field}")
    return found


def scan_extension(text):
    body = block(text, "pub struct Extension ")
    return {f for f in re.findall(r"^\s*pub (\w+):", body, re.M) if f != "dir"}


def scan_config(text):
    body = block(text, "pub struct Config ")
    return {f for f in re.findall(r"^\s*pub (\w+):", body, re.M) if f != "unknown"}


def scan_routes(text):
    found = set()
    for path, handler in re.findall(r'\.route\(\s*"([^"]+)",(.*?)\)\s*(?=\.route|\.fallback|\.layer|\.with_state)', text, re.S):
        for method in re.findall(r"\b(get|post|put|patch|delete)\(", handler):
            found.add(f"{method.upper()} {path}")
    return found


def scan_env(texts, excluded):
    names = set()
    for text in texts:
        names |= set(re.findall(r'"((?:LIGHTAGENT|HERMES)_[A-Z0-9_]+)"', text))
    def skip(name):
        return any(name == p or (p.endswith("*") and name.startswith(p[:-1])) for p in excluded)
    return {n for n in names if not skip(n)}


def rust_files():
    for base, _, files in os.walk(os.path.join(ROOT, "crates")):
        for name in files:
            if name.endswith(".rs"):
                with open(os.path.join(base, name), encoding="utf-8") as handle:
                    yield handle.read()


def compare(errors, label, found, listed):
    for item in sorted(found - listed):
        errors.append(f"{label}: source has {item!r} but no inventory row lists it")
    for item in sorted(listed - found):
        errors.append(f"{label}: inventory lists {item!r} but the source no longer has it")


def tokens(rows, kind):
    out = set()
    for row in rows:
        if row["kind"] == kind:
            out.update(row.get("tokens") or [])
    return out


def check(inventory, tree_lines, parity_md, sources, env_texts):
    errors = []
    rows = inventory["rows"]
    ids = [row.get("id") for row in rows]
    seen = set()
    for row in rows:
        for key in REQUIRED:
            if key not in row:
                errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: missing {key}")
        if row.get("id") in seen:
            errors.append(f"duplicate id {row.get('id')!r}")
        seen.add(row.get("id"))
        if row.get("kind") not in KINDS:
            errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: unknown kind {row.get('kind')!r}")
        if row.get("applicability") not in APPLICABILITY:
            errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: unknown applicability {row.get('applicability')!r}")
        if row.get("desktop_status") not in STATUS:
            errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: unknown status {row.get('desktop_status')!r}")
        if row.get("applicability") == "decision-D1" and not row.get("decision"):
            errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: a pending decision row must name its decision")
        if row.get("applicability") in ("not-applicable", "host-owned") and not (row.get("reason") or row.get("duplicate_of")):
            errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: {row['applicability']} needs a reason")
        if row.get("duplicate_of") and row["duplicate_of"] not in ids:
            errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: duplicate_of {row['duplicate_of']!r} is not a row")
        if row.get("desktop_status") == "implemented" and not (row.get("tested_sha") and row.get("actions_evidence")):
            errors.append(f"row {row.get('id')!r}: implemented without tested_sha and actions_evidence")
    decision_ids = {d["id"] for d in inventory.get("decisions", [])}
    for row in rows:
        if row.get("decision") and row["decision"] not in decision_ids:
            errors.append(f"row {row['id']!r}: decision {row['decision']!r} is not declared")

    keyed = [row["tree_key"] for row in rows if row.get("tree_key")]
    if len(keyed) != len(set(keyed)):
        errors.append("two rows share one cli-tree line")
    compare(errors, "cli-tree", set(tree_lines), set(keyed))

    compare(errors, "slash", scan_slash(sources["slash"]), tokens(rows, "slash"))
    compare(errors, "chat keys", scan_keys(sources["chat"]), tokens(rows, "keybinding"))
    compare(errors, "acp", scan_acp(sources["acp"]), tokens(rows, "acp-method"))
    compare(errors, "mcp", scan_mcp(sources["config"]), tokens(rows, "mcp-registration"))
    compare(errors, "extension manifest", scan_extension(sources["extensions"]), tokens(rows, "extension-registration"))
    compare(errors, "config sections", scan_config(sources["config"]), tokens(rows, "config"))
    routes = scan_routes(sources["api"]) | scan_routes(sources["provisioning"])
    compare(errors, "http routes", routes, tokens(rows, "api"))
    compare(errors, "env", scan_env(env_texts, inventory.get("excluded_env", [])), tokens(rows, "env"))

    counted = [r for r in rows if r.get("counts_toward_parity") and r.get("applicability") == "applicable"]
    implemented = sum(1 for r in counted if r.get("desktop_status") == "implemented")
    summary = inventory.get("summary", {})
    if summary.get("parity_denominator") != len(counted):
        errors.append(f"summary.parity_denominator is {summary.get('parity_denominator')}, rows give {len(counted)}")
    if summary.get("parity_implemented") != implemented:
        errors.append(f"summary.parity_implemented is {summary.get('parity_implemented')}, rows give {implemented}")
    if f"- Denominator: {len(counted)}" not in parity_md:
        errors.append(f"DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md does not state '- Denominator: {len(counted)}'")
    for row_id in ids:
        if f"`{row_id}`" not in parity_md:
            errors.append(f"DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md does not list `{row_id}`")
    return errors, len(counted), implemented


def load():
    with open(os.path.join(DOCS, "desktop-capabilities.json"), encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        inventory = json.load(handle)
    with open(os.path.join(DOCS, "cli-tree.txt"), encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        tree = [line for line in handle.read().splitlines() if line]
    with open(os.path.join(DOCS, "DESKTOP_CLI_PARITY.md"), encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        parity = handle.read()
    sources = {key: read(rel) for key, rel in SOURCES.items()}
    return inventory, tree, parity, sources, list(rust_files())


def self_test():
    inventory, tree, parity, sources, env_texts = load()
    base_errors, _, _ = check(inventory, tree, parity, sources, env_texts)
    if base_errors:
        print("self-test needs a clean baseline; the check itself fails:")
        print("\n".join(base_errors))
        return 1
    mutations = {
        "slash": lambda s: {**s, "slash": s["slash"].replace('"new" => Slash::New,', '"new" => Slash::New,\n        "frobnicate" => Slash::New,')},
        "chat keys": lambda s: {**s, "chat": s["chat"].replace("KeyCode::Home =>", "KeyCode::Tab => {}\n            KeyCode::Home =>", 1)},
        "acp": lambda s: {**s, "acp": s["acp"].replace('"session/cancel" => {', '"session/fork" => {}\n            "session/cancel" => {', 1)},
        "mcp": lambda s: {**s, "config": s["config"].replace("        url: String,\n", "        url: String,\n        proxy: String,\n", 1)},
        "extension manifest": lambda s: {**s, "extensions": s["extensions"].replace("    pub version: String,", "    pub version: String,\n    pub homepage: String,", 1)},
        "config sections": lambda s: {**s, "config": s["config"].replace("    pub inference: InferenceConfig,", "    pub inference: InferenceConfig,\n    pub telemetry: TelemetryConfig,", 1)},
        "http routes": lambda s: {**s, "api": s["api"].replace('.route("/health", get(health))', '.route("/health", get(health))\n        .route("/api/lightagent/v1/memory", get(health))', 1)},
    }
    failures = 0
    for label, mutate in mutations.items():
        changed = mutate(sources)
        if changed == sources:
            print(f"self-test: could not apply the {label} mutation (anchor moved)")
            failures += 1
            continue
        errors, _, _ = check(inventory, tree, parity, changed, env_texts)
        hit = [e for e in errors if e.startswith(label + ":")]
        print(f"self-test {label}: {'detected' if hit else 'MISSED'} {hit[:1]}")
        failures += 0 if hit else 1
    errors, _, _ = check(inventory, tree, parity, sources, env_texts + ['"LIGHTAGENT_NEW_KNOB"'])
    hit = [e for e in errors if e.startswith("env:")]
    print(f"self-test env: {'detected' if hit else 'MISSED'} {hit[:1]}")
    failures += 0 if hit else 1
    errors, _, _ = check(inventory, tree + ["cmd lightagent frobnicate"], parity, sources, env_texts)
    hit = [e for e in errors if e.startswith("cli-tree:")]
    print(f"self-test cli-tree: {'detected' if hit else 'MISSED'} {hit[:1]}")
    failures += 0 if hit else 1
    return 1 if failures else 0


def main():
    if "--self-test" in sys.argv:
        return self_test()
    inventory, tree, parity, sources, env_texts = load()
    errors, denominator, implemented = check(inventory, tree, parity, sources, env_texts)
    report = [
        "### Desktop capability inventory",
        f"- frozen at: `{inventory['frozen_at']['sha']}`",
        f"- rows: {len(inventory['rows'])}",
        f"- parity: {implemented} of {denominator} implemented",
        f"- pending D1 decisions: {', '.join(d['id'] for d in inventory.get('decisions', [])) or 'none'}",
        f"- drift errors: {len(errors)}",
    ]
    print("\n".join(report))
    if os.environ.get("GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"):
        with open(os.environ["GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"], "a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
            handle.write("\n".join(report) + "\n")
    for error in errors:
        print(f"::error::{error}")
    return 1 if errors else 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
=== write .github/workflows/desktop.yml
name: desktop

# Lightagent Desktop gates (docs/desktop/). Each slice adds its jobs here;
# everything runs on GitHub-hosted runners. D0: the capability inventory must
# match the source, and the drift check must prove it detects additions.

on:
  pull_request:
    paths:
      - 'crates/**'
      - 'frontend/**'
      - 'desktop/**'
      - 'docs/desktop/**'
      - 'scripts/check-desktop-inventory.py'
      - '.github/workflows/desktop.yml'
  workflow_dispatch:

concurrency:
  group: desktop-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

permissions:
  contents: read

env:
  CARGO_INCREMENTAL: '0'

jobs:
  inventory:
    name: desktop capability inventory
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04
    timeout-minutes: 40
    steps:
      # The exact PR head, not the synthetic merge commit, so the evidence
      # names the commit under review.
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with:
          ref: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha || github.sha }}
      - name: Record the tested commit
        run: |
          {
            echo "### Tested commit"
            echo "- head: \`$(git rev-parse HEAD)\`"
            echo "- tree: \`$(git rev-parse 'HEAD^{tree}')\`"
          } | tee -a "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
      - name: The drift check detects additions (self-test)
        run: python3 -I scripts/check-desktop-inventory.py --self-test
      - name: The inventory matches the source
        if: success() || failure()
        run: python3 -I scripts/check-desktop-inventory.py
      - run: rustup show
        if: success() || failure()
      - uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
        if: success() || failure()
        with:
          key: desktop-inventory
      - name: The CLI tree matches clap
        if: success() || failure()
        run: cargo test -p lightagent --lib --locked desktop_inventory
=== replace crates/lightagent/src/lib.rs
--- find
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
--- with
/// The clap command tree, pinned in `docs/desktop/cli-tree.txt` so the
/// desktop capability inventory notices every added or removed command,
/// option, argument, value or alias.
#[cfg(test)]
mod desktop_inventory {
    use clap::CommandFactory as _;

    fn walk(command: &clap::Command, path: &str, out: &mut Vec<String>) {
        for alias in command.get_visible_aliases() {
            out.push(format!("alias {path} = {alias}"));
        }
        for arg in command.get_arguments() {
            let id = arg.get_id().as_str();
            if id == "help" || id == "version" {
                continue;
            }
            let name = match arg.get_long() {
                Some(long) => format!("--{long}"),
                None => format!("<{id}>"),
            };
            out.push(format!("arg {path} {name}"));
            if arg.get_action().takes_values() {
                for value in arg.get_possible_values() {
                    if !value.is_hide_set() {
                        out.push(format!("value {path} {name} {}", value.get_name()));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        for sub in command.get_subcommands() {
            if sub.get_name() == "help" {
                continue;
            }
            let child = format!("{path} {}", sub.get_name());
            out.push(format!("cmd {child}"));
            walk(sub, &child, out);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_cli_tree_matches_the_desktop_inventory() {
        let mut out = vec!["cmd lightagent".to_owned()];
        walk(&super::Cli::command(), "lightagent", &mut out);
        out.sort();
        let actual = out.join("\n") + "\n";
        let expected = include_str!("../../../docs/desktop/cli-tree.txt").replace("\r\n", "\n");
        assert!(
            actual == expected,
            "the CLI changed: update docs/desktop/cli-tree.txt and the matching rows in \
             docs/desktop/desktop-capabilities.json\n----- actual cli tree -----\n{actual}----- end -----"
        );
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
=== end
